The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

Provenance bridgeUsed by the transform to answer:where did this claim come from?how much transcript backing exists?should this be surfaced as evidence or just as a weak lead?3. Semantic projection / embeddingsUsed by the transform to answer:what is semantically nearby across both curated and harvested material?what should be pulled into the candidate set before policy shaping?The boundary I would preserveRoles know:there is a recall functionit may search beyond the curated graphSteward mediates and shapes the answerreturned answers are not raw DB dumps but knowledge responsesRoles do not know / do not own:embedding store structurebridge-table semanticsranking heuristicsfederation plan constructionresult filtering / weighting internalsmutation/reconciliation rulesThat’s a very different kind of opacity than “you’re forbidden to ask.”So I’d revise my recommendation one more stepDon’t build a “Steward-only semantic search API.”Build asingle knowledge recall APIthat all roles use.But behind it, implement aSteward-owned recall enginewith these internal phases:Phase 1 — Candidate acquisitiongraph entity lookupsteward semantic document searchmaybe provenance/source expansionPhase 2 — Evidence expansionjoin toevidence_linksjoin to provenance/source artifactsattach supporting/contradicting harvest candidatesPhase 3 — Role transformrequester-role-specific weightingaccepted vs proposed evidence filteringgraph-first vs evidence-first shapingcontradiction handling rulesPhase 4 — Response shapingreturn a graph-safe knowledge packetnot raw blended recordsNet: I think this is the correct correctionYou’re not buildingsearch privilege.You’re buildingshared recall with Steward-owned internal query transformation.That’s better because it matches the rest of the system you’ve been describing:Steward is the sole semantic writerroles can request knowledgethe request goes through a policy/reconciliation boundaryNebula stays evidence/cabinet, not canonical truththe graph remains the committed state, but the recall path can consult more than the graphIf you want, I can now write the spec in the corrected form:Steward Recall Transformation Layer Spec v0with:shared publicKnowledgeRecallRequest/KnowledgeRecallResultinternalStewardRecallOrchestratorevidence_links,source_artifacts,semantic_documentsrole-based transformation pipelineaccepted/proposed evidence handlingMCP contract for “everyone can ask, Steward mediates”That’s probably the right artifact now, because the boundary has shifted fromwho may calltowhat gets injected into the call path.SourcesChatGPT can make mistakes. │ erences   │ (CIRS→projection algebra, etc.)            │ domain system, agent_record → plan, etc.)        │
│ Data      │ Tables exist, may be empty (migration      │ 403 candidates classified across 10 domains with │
│ status    │ never run?)                                │ 719 cross-references                             │
└───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).
Want me to start building any of these linkages?Show moreShow lessChatGPT said:Thought for 7sYes — and I’d do them in a very specific order, because you’ve already told me two constraints that matter:Nebula is not brain— it’s the cabinet / evidence / harvest substrate, not the authoritative semantic layer.You don’t want to throw the current KG away or let auto-graph mutation run wild yet— so anything we do should preserve provenance and keep graph writes gated.Given that, I wouldnotstart with “unified semantic search” as the first move, even though it’s the flashiest. I’d start by building the bridge that makes the later searchcorrectinstead of just convenient.What I’d build firstPhase 1 — Cross-schema evidence bridgeBuild this first.WhyRight now the core missing thing is not “search,” it’ssemantic adjacency with provenance:harvest candidate says somethingcurated knowledge graph contains a concept / decision / entitythere is no durable, queryable statement that“this harvested claim supports / refines / contradicts / instantiates that knowledge entity.”Without that bridge:embeddings are just floating similarityunified search returns “related stuff” but notwhy it is relatedyou can’t safely rebuild or evolve the KG from harvest evidence laterSteward has no principled substrate to adjudicate fromThis fits your architecture better because it keepsNebula as evidenceandKnowledge/Steward as semantic interpretation, instead of collapsing them.The order I recommend1) Add cross-schema cross-referencesGoalCreate a durable relation from Nebula artifacts to Knowledge entities.New tableI would add a bridge table inknowledge, not nebula, because this is asemantic interpretationof evidence, not a property of the harvest itself.Something like:SQLknowledge.evidence_links-id uuid pk-knowledge_entity_id uuidnotnull-nebula_harvest_id uuidnull-nebula_candidate_id uuidnull-link_type textnotnull-- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | supersedes | mentions-confidencenumeric(5,4)null-rationale textnull-created_by textnotnull-- 'steward_ingestor_v1', 'manual', 'reconciler'-source_span jsonbnull-- optional offsets, excerpt ids, transcript turn refs-metadata jsonbnotnulldefault'{}'-created_at timestamptznotnulldefaultnow()InvariantsNebula remains immutable evidence-ish history; The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).

You said:Current State: Two Schemas, Adjacent but Unlinked
┌───────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           │ Knowledge ("memories")                     │ Nebula ("cabinet")                               │
├───────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Schema    │ knowledge.*                                │ nebula.*                                         │
│ Tables    │ graph_entities, graph_edges,               │ harvests_history, agent_records_history,         │
│           │ graph_cross_references, graph_migrations   │ cross_references, systems_history,               │
│           │                                            │ requirements_history, etc.                       │
│ Has       │ ✅ embedding VECTOR(1536) + ivfflat index  │ ❌ No embeddings                                 │
│ vector    │ + semantic_search() function               │                                                  │
│ search?   │                                            │                                                  │
│ Source    │ nexus/graph/nexus-knowledge-graph.json     │ 30 harvests from chat transcripts (automated     │
│ material  │ (curated, 1 big file)                      │ pipeline)                                        │
│ Ingestion │ migrate_graph.py (one-shot file → DB)      │ batch_harvest_to_db.py (per-transcript → Ollama  │
│ method    │                                            │ → DB)                                            │
│ MCP       │ nexus/typescript/knowledge-mcp/            │ nexus/typescript/nebula-mcp/ + nebula-srv REST   │
│ server    │ (knowledge_list_entities,                  │ API                                              │
│           │ knowledge_semantic_search, etc.)           │                                                  │
│ Cross-ref │ knowledge.graph_cross_references           │ nebula.cross_references (harvest_candidate →     │
│ erences   │ (CIRS→projection algebra, etc.)            │ domain system, agent_record → plan, etc.)        │
│ Data      │ Tables exist, may be empty (migration      │ 403 candidates classified across 10 domains with │
│ status    │ never run?)                                │ 719 cross-references                             │
└───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).
Want me to start building any of these linkages?Show moreShow less

│ Tables    │ graph_entities, graph_edges,               │ harvests_history, agent_records_history,         │
│           │ graph_cross_references, graph_migrations   │ cross_references, systems_history,               │
│           │                                            │ requirements_history, etc.                       │
│ Has       │ ✅ embedding VECTOR(1536) + ivfflat index  │ ❌ No embeddings                                 │
│ vector    │ + semantic_search() function               │                                                  │
│ search?   │                                            │                                                  │
│ Source    │ nexus/graph/nexus-knowledge-graph.json     │ 30 harvests from chat transcripts (automated     │
│ material  │ (curated, 1 big file)                      │ pipeline)                                        │
│ Ingestion │ migrate_graph.py (one-shot file → DB)      │ batch_harvest_to_db.py (per-transcript → Ollama  │
│ method    │                                            │ → DB)                                            │
│ MCP       │ nexus/typescript/knowledge-mcp/            │ nexus/typescript/nebula-mcp/ + nebula-srv REST   │
│ server    │ (knowledge_list_entities,                  │ API                                              │
│           │ knowledge_semantic_search, etc.)           │                                                  │
│ Cross-ref │ knowledge.graph_cross_references           │ nebula.cross_references (harvest_candidate →     │
│ erences   │ (CIRS→projection algebra, etc.)            │ domain system, agent_record → plan, etc.)        │
│ Data      │ Tables exist, may be empty (migration      │ 403 candidates classified across 10 domains with │
│ status    │ never run?)                                │ 719 cross-references                             │
└───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).
Want me to start building any of these linkages?Show moreShow less

│ method    │                                            │ → DB)                                            │
│ MCP       │ nexus/typescript/knowledge-mcp/            │ nexus/typescript/nebula-mcp/ + nebula-srv REST   │
│ server    │ (knowledge_list_entities,                  │ API                                              │
│           │ knowledge_semantic_search, etc.)           │                                                  │
│ Cross-ref │ knowledge.graph_cross_references           │ nebula.cross_references (harvest_candidate →     │
│ erences   │ (CIRS→projection algebra, etc.)            │ domain system, agent_record → plan, etc.)        │
│ Data      │ Tables exist, may be empty (migration      │ 403 candidates classified across 10 domains with │
│ status    │ never run?)                                │ 719 cross-references                             │
└───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
The Overlap (Where They Converge)
They describe the same domain from different angles:
- Knowledge graph entities like  persistent-knowledge-graph  (section:  types ) describe the concept of a knowledge graph in PostgreSQL+pgvector
- Nebula harvest candidates are the actual output of applying that concept — extracted specs sitting in  harvests_history.candidates 
Same architectural decisions appear in both:
- "TypeSpec as executable knowledge layer" appears as a harvest candidate AND as a knowledge graph entity ( typespec-dsl-layer )
- "3-tier graph model" appears as a knowledge graph  architectural_observation  AND in harvested specs
The Four Missing Linkages
1. Cross-Schema Cross-References
 nebula.cross_references  links  harvest_candidate → system  (within nebula). There's no link from a harvest candidate to the knowledge graph entity it instantiates or refines. A candidate about "TypeSpec as executable knowledge" should reference the  typespec-dsl-layer  entity in  knowledge.graph_entities .
2. Embedding Pipeline
Harvest candidates have rich  intent_description  text — perfect for embedding. But no embedding generation pipeline exists. The Rover schema ( rover.chunks ) was designed with  nlp_candidate_json  and  nlp_verdict  columns but those tables were never created in the production database. Harvest source text is stored in  harvests_history.source_text  but never chunked, embedded, or indexed for semantic search.
3. Source Provenance Bridge
 knowledge.graph_entities.source_file  =  nexus-knowledge-graph.json .  nebula.harvests_history.source_filename  = e.g.,  Nexus - OrientDB, Pinecone & Convex.html . The knowledge graph JSON has a  sources  array pointing to chat transcripts. A bridge would let you go from a harvest candidate → the knowledge graph entities it informs, and vice versa.
4. Unified Semantic Search
The  knowledge.semantic_search()  function exists but is a stub (requires application-layer embedding generation). If harvest candidates were embedded and stored in the knowledge schema (or had a vector column in nebula), you could run: "find all harvest candidates semantically related to 'TypeSpec contract-first architecture'" — and get results from both the curated knowledge graph AND the automated harvest pipeline.
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Bottom line: The schemas exist side-by-side. The MCP servers exist. The data exists. What's missing is the bridge — cross-schema references, embeddings on harvest data, and a unified semantic search that spans both "memories" (curated graph) and "cabinet contents" (harvested transcripts).
Want me to start building any of these linkages?

I’d start by building the bridge that makes the later searchcorrectinstead of just convenient.What I’d build firstPhase 1 — Cross-schema evidence bridgeBuild this first.WhyRight now the core missing thing is not “search,” it’ssemantic adjacency with provenance:harvest candidate says somethingcurated knowledge graph contains a concept / decision / entitythere is no durable, queryable statement that“this harvested claim supports / refines / contradicts / instantiates that knowledge entity.”Without that bridge:embeddings are just floating similarityunified search returns “related stuff” but notwhy it is relatedyou can’t safely rebuild or evolve the KG from harvest evidence laterSteward has no principled substrate to adjudicate fromThis fits your architecture better because it keepsNebula as evidenceandKnowledge/Steward as semantic interpretation, instead of collapsing them.The order I recommend1) Add cross-schema cross-referencesGoalCreate a durable relation from Nebula artifacts to Knowledge entities.New tableI would add a bridge table inknowledge, not nebula, because this is asemantic interpretationof evidence, not a property of the harvest itself.Something like:SQLknowledge.evidence_links-id uuid pk-knowledge_entity_id uuidnotnull-nebula_harvest_id uuidnull-nebula_candidate_id uuidnull-link_type textnotnull-- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | supersedes | mentions-confidencenumeric(5,4)null-rationale textnull-created_by textnotnull-- 'steward_ingestor_v1', 'manual', 'reconciler'-source_span jsonbnull-- optional offsets, excerpt ids, transcript turn refs-metadata jsonbnotnulldefault'{}'-created_at timestamptznotnulldefaultnow()InvariantsNebula remains immutable evidence-ish history; I’d start by building the bridge that makes the later searchcorrectinstead of just convenient.What I’d build firstPhase 1 — Cross-schema evidence bridgeBuild this first.WhyRight now the core missing thing is not “search,” it’ssemantic adjacency with provenance:harvest candidate says somethingcurated knowledge graph contains a concept / decision / entitythere is no durable, queryable statement that“this harvested claim supports / refines / contradicts / instantiates that knowledge entity.”Without that bridge:embeddings are just floating similarityunified search returns “related stuff” but notwhy it is relatedyou can’t safely rebuild or evolve the KG from harvest evidence laterSteward has no principled substrate to adjudicate fromThis fits your architecture better because it keepsNebula as evidenceandKnowledge/Steward as semantic interpretation, instead of collapsing them.The order I recommend1) Add cross-schema cross-referencesGoalCreate a durable relation from Nebula artifacts to Knowledge entities.New tableI would add a bridge table inknowledge, not nebula, because this is asemantic interpretationof evidence, not a property of the harvest itself.Something like:SQLknowledge.evidence_links-id uuid pk-knowledge_entity_id uuidnotnull-nebula_harvest_id uuidnull-nebula_candidate_id uuidnull-link_type textnotnull-- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | supersedes | mentions-confidencenumeric(5,4)null-rationale textnull-created_by textnotnull-- 'steward_ingestor_v1', 'manual', 'reconciler'-source_span jsonbnull-- optional offsets, excerpt ids, transcript turn refs-metadata jsonbnotnulldefault'{}'-created_at timestamptznotnulldefaultnow()InvariantsNebula remains immutable evidence-ish history; this table does not rewrite harvest records.A link is aninterpretation layer artifact, not a source artifact.Multiple candidates can link to one knowledge entity.One candidate can link to multiple knowledge entities with different link types.Links are additive and versionable; don’t silently overwrite them.Minimal first passSupport only these link types initially:supportsrefinesinstantiatescontradictsThat’s enough to make the system useful without ontology bloat.2) Add provenance/source bridgeGoalLet you walk both directions:harvest candidate → transcript / harvest / entityknowledge entity → source transcript(s) / harvest candidate(s) that informed itThis is where the JSON KG’ssources[]and Nebula’ssource_filename/ harvest IDs get normalized into one navigable layer.Why this is secondBecause once evidence links exist, provenance becomes straightforward and extremely valuable.You’ll want to answer questions like:“show me every transcript fragment that contributed totypespec-dsl-layer”“which harvested candidates fed this architectural observation?”“which KG entities are currently backed only by legacy JSON and not by transcript harvests?”What I’d addA normalized provenance model rather than trying to infer everything from filenames.Possible table:SQLknowledge.source_artifacts-id uuid pk-source_kind textnotnull-- transcript_html | transcript_turn | harvest | harvest_candidate | kg_json_section-external_key textnotnull-source_filename textnull-metadata jsonbnotnulldefault'{}'and then either:useknowledge.evidence_linksdirectly to point at source artifacts, oraddknowledge.entity_sources(entity_id, source_artifact_id, relation_type, ...)If I were optimizing for speed, I’dskip a full generalized source model on day oneand just enrichevidence_linkswith enough source identifiers to get the job done. Then normalize later.3) Add embeddings for harvest candidatesGoalMake Nebula content semantically retrievablewithoutpretending it is canonical knowledge.Why this is third, not firstBecause embeddings without linkage create a fancy sidecar search index.Embeddingsafterlinkage become genuinely useful:retrieve candidateshow which KG entities it supports/refinescluster evidence around an entitysurface candidate-to-entity match suggestions for Steward reviewMy recommendation on where to store themDonotbury this in Nebula if your long-term plan is Steward-mediated semantic operations.I’d create ashared semantic projection layerrather than adding ad hoc vectors directly tonebula.*tables.Two viable approaches:Option A — Preferred: a sharedknowledge.semantic_documents/knowledge.semantic_chunksThis is the cleanest fit with your architecture.ExampleSQLknowledge.semantic_documents-id uuid pk-origin_system textnotnull-- 'knowledge' | 'nebula'-origin_table textnotnull-origin_id uuidnotnull-document_kind textnotnull-- knowledge_entity | harvest_candidate | harvest_source_chunk-title textnull-body textnotnull-metadata jsonbnotnulldefault'{}'-embedding vector(1536)null-created_at timestamptznotnulldefaultnow()-updated_at timestamptznotnulldefaultnow()Optionally split outsemantic_chunksif you want chunk-level indexing.Why I like thisIt gives you one semantic index across multiple systemswithout pretending the systems are the same thing.So the semantic layer becomes a projection over:knowledge.graph_entitiesnebula.harvests_historynebula.agent_records_historymaybe later PEB docs, requirements, incidents, etc.That lines up very well with the way you’ve been describing Steward as a federator / interpreter rather than Nebula as source of truth.Option B — Simpler but less clean: add vector columns to Nebula candidate tablesThis is faster if you just want semantic search tomorrow, but I think it’s architecturally weaker.I’d only do this if you explicitly want aNebula-local search capabilitybefore the shared projection exists.4) Build unified semantic searchThis should comeafterthe above, because then it’s not “search two piles of text,” it’s “search a semantic fabric with evidence trails.”The query surface I’d wantA single search endpoint from Steward / Knowledge MCP that can search across both layers and tell you what you got back.Example response categories:knowledge_entityharvest_candidateharvest_source_chunkagent_recordlater:open_question,incident,requirement, etc.Query behaviorA search for:“TypeSpec contract-first architecture”should be able to return:the KG entity fortypespec-dsl-layerrelated harvest candidatestranscript chunks that mention itevidence links showing which candidates support or refine the entitymaybe unresolved contradictions or competing formulationsThat’s aSteward-gradequery, not just vector search.My concrete recommendation: the next slice to implementIf you want me to pick the next engineering artifact, I’d have you buildthis exact slice first:Bridge Slice v0Deliverables1) New bridge tableknowledge.evidence_links2) Matching jobA job/service that proposes candidate ↔ entity links:InputNebula harvest candidatesKnowledge graph entitiesSignalsexact / fuzzy title overlapentity aliases / keywordsdomain matchtranscript source overlapembedding similarityif available latercross-reference hints already present in nebulaOutputproposedsupports/refines/instantiates/contradictslinksconfidence + rationale3) Review/apply pathstore proposals separately or as low-confidence evidence linksSteward / manual review can promote themno silent mutation of KG entities4) Query surfaceAdd two queries:knowledge_get_entity_evidence(entity_id)→ all linked harvest candidates + provenancenebula_get_candidate_knowledge_links(candidate_id)→ all linked knowledge entities + link typesThat alone gives you the first real bridge between “cabinet” and “memory.”What I wouldnotdo firstI would not start with “embed everything and search it”Because you’ll end up with a very seductive but semantically mushy layer:useful retrievalweak explainabilityunclear ownershipno clean path from “this looks related” to “this updates the graph under governance”That’s exactly the kind of thing that becomes expensive later when you want Steward to reason over it.If you want the shortest path to visible valueThen I’d do thistwo-step implementation plan:Step A — Evidence bridge firstcreateknowledge.evidence_linkswritebridge_harvest_candidates.pygenerate candidate→entity proposalsexpose MCP read methodsStep B — Semantic projection secondcreateknowledge.semantic_documentsbackfill:knowledge.graph_entitiesnebula.harvest_candidatesimplement a singlesemantic_search_union(query, filters)over that projectioninclude evidence-link joins in resultsThat gets you:preserved separation of concernsprovenanceexplainable searchno premature auto-graph mutationa foundation for later “harvest → steward DB direct ingest”My answer to your “want me to start building any of these?” questionYes: start with Linkage #1, but implement it as a Steward-side evidence bridge, not a Nebula-local convenience feature.Then do#3 embeddingsin ashared semantic projection layer, and only then expose#4 unified semantic search.If you want, I can do the next step right now as an actual engineering artifact:I can write“Knowledge–Nebula Evidence Bridge Spec v0”with:schema DDLtable contracts and invariantslink type ontologycandidate→entity matching algorithmconfidence scoring rulesMCP endpointsbackfill / migration planphased rollout planIf you say go, I’ll write it in the same style as the WRP package spec: classes, contracts, invariants, SQL, and pseudocode.

Yes — and I’d do them in a very specific order, because you’ve already told me two constraints that matter:

- Nebula is not brain— it’s the cabinet / evidence / harvest substrate, not the authoritative semantic layer.
Nebula is not brain— it’s the cabinet / evidence / harvest substrate, not the authoritative semantic layer.

- You don’t want to throw the current KG away or let auto-graph mutation run wild yet— so anything we do should preserve provenance and keep graph writes gated.
You don’t want to throw the current KG away or let auto-graph mutation run wild yet— so anything we do should preserve provenance and keep graph writes gated.

Given that, I wouldnotstart with “unified semantic search” as the first move, even though it’s the flashiest. I’d start by building the bridge that makes the later searchcorrectinstead of just convenient.

# What I’d build first

## Phase 1 — Cross-schema evidence bridge

Build this first.

### Why

Right now the core missing thing is not “search,” it’ssemantic adjacency with provenance:

- harvest candidate says something
harvest candidate says something

- curated knowledge graph contains a concept / decision / entity
curated knowledge graph contains a concept / decision / entity

- there is no durable, queryable statement that“this harvested claim supports / refines / contradicts / instantiates that knowledge entity.”
there is no durable, queryable statement that“this harvested claim supports / refines / contradicts / instantiates that knowledge entity.”

Without that bridge:

- embeddings are just floating similarity
embeddings are just floating similarity

- unified search returns “related stuff” but notwhy it is related
unified search returns “related stuff” but notwhy it is related

- you can’t safely rebuild or evolve the KG from harvest evidence later
you can’t safely rebuild or evolve the KG from harvest evidence later

- Steward has no principled substrate to adjudicate from
Steward has no principled substrate to adjudicate from

This fits your architecture better because it keepsNebula as evidenceandKnowledge/Steward as semantic interpretation, instead of collapsing them.

---

# The order I recommend

## 1) Add cross-schema cross-references

### Goal

Create a durable relation from Nebula artifacts to Knowledge entities.

### New table

I would add a bridge table inknowledge, not nebula, because this is asemantic interpretationof evidence, not a property of the harvest itself.

Something like:

```
knowledge.evidence_links- id uuid pk- knowledge_entity_id uuid not null- nebula_harvest_id uuid null- nebula_candidate_id uuid null- link_type text not null    -- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | supersedes | mentions- confidence numeric(5,4) null- rationale text null- created_by text not null    -- 'steward_ingestor_v1', 'manual', 'reconciler'- source_span jsonb null    -- optional offsets, excerpt ids, transcript turn refs- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'- created_at timestamptz not null default now()
```

```
knowledge.evidence_links- id uuid pk- knowledge_entity_id uuid not null- nebula_harvest_id uuid null- nebula_candidate_id uuid null- link_type text not null    -- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | supersedes | mentions- confidence numeric(5,4) null- rationale text null- created_by text not null    -- 'steward_ingestor_v1', 'manual', 'reconciler'- source_span jsonb null    -- optional offsets, excerpt ids, transcript turn refs- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'- created_at timestamptz not null default now()
```

### Invariants

- Nebula remains immutable evidence-ish history; this table does not rewrite harvest records.
Nebula remains immutable evidence-ish history; this table does not rewrite harvest records.

- A link is aninterpretation layer artifact, not a source artifact.
A link is aninterpretation layer artifact, not a source artifact.

- Multiple candidates can link to one knowledge entity.
Multiple candidates can link to one knowledge entity.

- One candidate can link to multiple knowledge entities with different link types.
One candidate can link to multiple knowledge entities with different link types.

- Links are additive and versionable; don’t silently overwrite them.
Links are additive and versionable; don’t silently overwrite them.

### Minimal first pass

Support only these link types initially:

- supports
supports

- refines
refines

- instantiates
instantiates

- contradicts
contradicts

That’s enough to make the system useful without ontology bloat.

---

## 2) Add provenance/source bridge

### Goal

Let you walk both directions:

- harvest candidate → transcript / harvest / entity
harvest candidate → transcript / harvest / entity

- knowledge entity → source transcript(s) / harvest candidate(s) that informed it
knowledge entity → source transcript(s) / harvest candidate(s) that informed it

This is where the JSON KG’ssources[]and Nebula’ssource_filename/ harvest IDs get normalized into one navigable layer.

### Why this is second

Because once evidence links exist, provenance becomes straightforward and extremely valuable.You’ll want to answer questions like:

- “show me every transcript fragment that contributed totypespec-dsl-layer”
“show me every transcript fragment that contributed totypespec-dsl-layer”

- “which harvested candidates fed this architectural observation?”
“which harvested candidates fed this architectural observation?”

- “which KG entities are currently backed only by legacy JSON and not by transcript harvests?”
“which KG entities are currently backed only by legacy JSON and not by transcript harvests?”

### What I’d add

A normalized provenance model rather than trying to infer everything from filenames.

Possible table:

```
knowledge.source_artifacts- id uuid pk- source_kind text not null    -- transcript_html | transcript_turn | harvest | harvest_candidate | kg_json_section- external_key text not null- source_filename text null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'
```

```
knowledge.source_artifacts- id uuid pk- source_kind text not null    -- transcript_html | transcript_turn | harvest | harvest_candidate | kg_json_section- external_key text not null- source_filename text null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'
```

and then either:

- useknowledge.evidence_linksdirectly to point at source artifacts, or
useknowledge.evidence_linksdirectly to point at source artifacts, or

- addknowledge.entity_sources(entity_id, source_artifact_id, relation_type, ...)
addknowledge.entity_sources(entity_id, source_artifact_id, relation_type, ...)

If I were optimizing for speed, I’dskip a full generalized source model on day oneand just enrichevidence_linkswith enough source identifiers to get the job done. Then normalize later.

---

## 3) Add embeddings for harvest candidates

### Goal

Make Nebula content semantically retrievablewithoutpretending it is canonical knowledge.

### Why this is third, not first

Because embeddings without linkage create a fancy sidecar search index.Embeddingsafterlinkage become genuinely useful:

- retrieve candidate
retrieve candidate

- show which KG entities it supports/refines
show which KG entities it supports/refines

- cluster evidence around an entity
cluster evidence around an entity

- surface candidate-to-entity match suggestions for Steward review
surface candidate-to-entity match suggestions for Steward review

### My recommendation on where to store them

Donotbury this in Nebula if your long-term plan is Steward-mediated semantic operations.

I’d create ashared semantic projection layerrather than adding ad hoc vectors directly tonebula.*tables.

Two viable approaches:

---

## Option A — Preferred: a sharedknowledge.semantic_documents/knowledge.semantic_chunks

This is the cleanest fit with your architecture.

### Example

```
knowledge.semantic_documents- id uuid pk- origin_system text not null    -- 'knowledge' | 'nebula'- origin_table text not null- origin_id uuid not null- document_kind text not null    -- knowledge_entity | harvest_candidate | harvest_source_chunk- title text null- body text not null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'- embedding vector(1536) null- created_at timestamptz not null default now()- updated_at timestamptz not null default now()
```

```
knowledge.semantic_documents- id uuid pk- origin_system text not null    -- 'knowledge' | 'nebula'- origin_table text not null- origin_id uuid not null- document_kind text not null    -- knowledge_entity | harvest_candidate | harvest_source_chunk- title text null- body text not null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'- embedding vector(1536) null- created_at timestamptz not null default now()- updated_at timestamptz not null default now()
```

Optionally split outsemantic_chunksif you want chunk-level indexing.

### Why I like this

It gives you one semantic index across multiple systemswithout pretending the systems are the same thing.

So the semantic layer becomes a projection over:

- knowledge.graph_entities
knowledge.graph_entities

- nebula.harvests_history
nebula.harvests_history

- nebula.agent_records_history
nebula.agent_records_history

- maybe later PEB docs, requirements, incidents, etc.
maybe later PEB docs, requirements, incidents, etc.

That lines up very well with the way you’ve been describing Steward as a federator / interpreter rather than Nebula as source of truth.

---

## Option B — Simpler but less clean: add vector columns to Nebula candidate tables

This is faster if you just want semantic search tomorrow, but I think it’s architecturally weaker.

I’d only do this if you explicitly want aNebula-local search capabilitybefore the shared projection exists.

---

## 4) Build unified semantic search

This should comeafterthe above, because then it’s not “search two piles of text,” it’s “search a semantic fabric with evidence trails.”

### The query surface I’d want

A single search endpoint from Steward / Knowledge MCP that can search across both layers and tell you what you got back.

Example response categories:

- knowledge_entity
knowledge_entity

- harvest_candidate
harvest_candidate

- harvest_source_chunk
harvest_source_chunk

- agent_record
agent_record

- later:open_question,incident,requirement, etc.
later:open_question,incident,requirement, etc.

### Query behavior

A search for:

> “TypeSpec contract-first architecture”

“TypeSpec contract-first architecture”

should be able to return:

- the KG entity fortypespec-dsl-layer
the KG entity fortypespec-dsl-layer

- related harvest candidates
related harvest candidates

- transcript chunks that mention it
transcript chunks that mention it

- evidence links showing which candidates support or refine the entity
evidence links showing which candidates support or refine the entity

- maybe unresolved contradictions or competing formulations
maybe unresolved contradictions or competing formulations

That’s aSteward-gradequery, not just vector search.

## Deliverables

### 1) New bridge table

knowledge.evidence_links

### 2) Matching job

A job/service that proposes candidate ↔ entity links:

Input

- Nebula harvest candidates
Nebula harvest candidates

- Knowledge graph entities
Knowledge graph entities

Signals

- exact / fuzzy title overlap
exact / fuzzy title overlap

- entity aliases / keywords
entity aliases / keywords

domain match

transcript source overlap

embedding similarityif available later

cross-reference hints already present in nebula

proposedsupports/refines/instantiates/contradictslinks

confidence + rationale

### 3) Review/apply path

- store proposals separately or as low-confidence evidence links
store proposals separately or as low-confidence evidence links

- Steward / manual review can promote them
Steward / manual review can promote them

- no silent mutation of KG entities
no silent mutation of KG entities

### 4) Query surface

Add two queries:

- knowledge_get_entity_evidence(entity_id)→ all linked harvest candidates + provenance
knowledge_get_entity_evidence(entity_id)→ all linked harvest candidates + provenance

- nebula_get_candidate_knowledge_links(candidate_id)→ all linked knowledge entities + link types
nebula_get_candidate_knowledge_links(candidate_id)→ all linked knowledge entities + link types

That alone gives you the first real bridge between “cabinet” and “memory.”

# What I wouldnotdo first

Because you’ll end up with a very seductive but semantically mushy layer:

- useful retrieval
useful retrieval

- weak explainability
weak explainability

- unclear ownership
unclear ownership

- no clean path from “this looks related” to “this updates the graph under governance”
no clean path from “this looks related” to “this updates the graph under governance”

That’s exactly the kind of thing that becomes expensive later when you want Steward to reason over it.
