Yes — that changes the shape of the plan in a good way.What you’re really saying is:Build the substrate now, but reserve thefederated read pathas a Steward capability.Other roles can keep using their normal graph/Nebula functions and never get a raw “search everything everywhere” bypass.I think that’s the right boundary.Revised plan: do1 + 2 + 3 now, but4 lives inside StewardThe governance ruleNebula MCPremains cabinet-facing: harvests, candidates, cross-refs, source material.Knowledge MCPremains graph-facing: entities, edges, graph refs, curated semantic operations.Knowledge Stewardbecomes theonlyrole allowed to executecross-surface semantic federation.So the system architecture becomes:Other roles├─ can query Knowledge through graph functions├─ can query Nebula through cabinet functions└─ cannot issue “search both and reconcile” directlyKnowledge Steward├─ can read Knowledge├─ can read Nebula├─ can use evidence bridge tables├─ can use semantic projection / embeddings└─ owns unified semantic search + reconciliation suggestionsThat gives you exactly the control boundary you want:the bridge exists in the substrate, but the bypass does not exist as a public capability.I’d now define the four linkages like this1) Cross-schema cross-referencesBuild nowBut I’d slightly rename the concept in implementation to make the intent clearer:not“cross-schema refs” in the generic senseyes“evidence links” / “knowledge evidence bridge”PurposeRepresent:a Nebula harvest candidateor harvest/source fragmentasevidence abouta knowledge entityOwnershipThis should be owned byknowledge/steward-side schema, not Nebula.WhyBecause the statement:“harvest candidate X refines knowledge entity Y”isnot a property of the harvest. It’s a semantic judgment made by the steward/knowledge layer.2) Provenance bridgeBuild nowThis is the second thing I’d do because it makes the evidence links auditable.PurposeSupport these traversals:knowledge entity -> supporting harvest candidates -> originating transcriptharvest candidate -> transcript source -> linked knowledge entitiesImportant distinctionI would keeptwo different kinds of relationshipseparate:A.Evidence link“this candidate supports/refines/contradicts entity X”B.Provenance link“this candidate came from transcript T / harvest H / span S”That separation matters because later you’ll want to ask:“show me all evidence for this entity”“show me where this candidate came from”“show me evidence that comes from this transcript”Those are related, but not the same edge.3) Embedding pipelineBuild nowBut I would not think of this as “add embeddings to Nebula.”I’d think of it as:Build a Steward-owned semantic projection layer over both systemsThat gives you the bridge without giving every role direct access to it.The architecture I’d useA. Keep the source systems separateknowledge.*remains curated graph / semantic memorynebula.*remains harvested cabinet / transcript-derived artifactsB. Add aSteward semantic workspaceSomething like one of these:knowledge_steward.*steward.*orknowledge.*if you want to keep it physically in the knowledge schemaI’d lean toward a dedicated schema if you want the boundary to stay obvious:steward.*Because the moment you say “only Steward gets the unified path,” it becomes useful to give Steward its own persistence layer for:bridge linkssearch Yes — that changes the shape of the plan in a good way.What you’re really saying is:Build the substrate now, but reserve thefederated read pathas a Steward capability.Other roles can keep using their normal graph/Nebula functions and never get a raw “search everything everywhere” bypass.I think that’s the right boundary.Revised plan: do1 + 2 + 3 now, but4 lives inside StewardThe governance ruleNebula MCPremains cabinet-facing: harvests, candidates, cross-refs, source material.Knowledge MCPremains graph-facing: entities, edges, graph refs, curated semantic operations.Knowledge Stewardbecomes theonlyrole allowed to executecross-surface semantic federation.So the system architecture becomes:Other roles├─ can query Knowledge through graph functions├─ can query Nebula through cabinet functions└─ cannot issue “search both and reconcile” directlyKnowledge Steward├─ can read Knowledge├─ can read Nebula├─ can use evidence bridge tables├─ can use semantic projection / embeddings└─ owns unified semantic search + reconciliation suggestionsThat gives you exactly the control boundary you want:the bridge exists in the substrate, but the bypass does not exist as a public capability.I’d now define the four linkages like this1) Cross-schema cross-referencesBuild nowBut I’d slightly rename the concept in implementation to make the intent clearer:not“cross-schema refs” in the generic senseyes“evidence links” / “knowledge evidence bridge”PurposeRepresent:a Nebula harvest candidateor harvest/source fragmentasevidence abouta knowledge entityOwnershipThis should be owned byknowledge/steward-side schema, not Nebula.WhyBecause the statement:“harvest candidate X refines knowledge entity Y”isnot a property of the harvest. It’s a semantic judgment made by the steward/knowledge layer.2) Provenance bridgeBuild nowThis is the second thing I’d do because it makes the evidence links auditable.PurposeSupport these traversals:knowledge entity -> supporting harvest candidates -> originating transcriptharvest candidate -> transcript source -> linked knowledge entitiesImportant distinctionI would keeptwo different kinds of relationshipseparate:A.Evidence link“this candidate supports/refines/contradicts entity X”B.Provenance link“this candidate came from transcript T / harvest H / span S”That separation matters because later you’ll want to ask:“show me all evidence for this entity”“show me where this candidate came from”“show me evidence that comes from this transcript”Those are related, but not the same edge.3) Embedding pipelineBuild nowBut I would not think of this as “add embeddings to Nebula.”I’d think of it as:Build a Steward-owned semantic projection layer over both systemsThat gives you the bridge without giving every role direct access to it.The architecture I’d useA. Keep the source systems separateknowledge.*remains curated graph / semantic memorynebula.*remains harvested cabinet / transcript-derived artifactsB. Add aSteward semantic workspaceSomething like one of these:knowledge_steward.*steward.*orknowledge.*if you want to keep it physically in the knowledge schemaI’d lean toward a dedicated schema if you want the boundary to stay obvious:steward.*Because the moment you say “only Steward gets the unified path,” it becomes useful to give Steward its own persistence layer for:bridge linkssearch projectionsreconciliation proposalsadjudication stateProposed schema splitKeep existingknowledge.graph_entitiesknowledge.graph_edgesknowledge.graph_cross_referencesnebula.harvests_historynebula.cross_referencesnebula.agent_records_historyetc.Add steward-owned bridge/projection like they should be linked / merged / updated.”Why I prefer asteward.*schema over stuffing this intoknowledge.*Because your requirement is not just technical — it’sconstitutional.You want:graph function, not a shared utilitySo I would explicitlynotimplement:knowledge.semantic_search_union(...)as a public Knowledge MCP functionnebula.semantic_search_union(...)a shared helper every role can Yes — that changes the shape of the plan in a good way.

What you’re really saying is:

> Build the substrate now, but reserve thefederated read pathas a Steward capability.Other roles can keep using their normal graph/Nebula functions and never get a raw “search everything everywhere” bypass.

Build the substrate now, but reserve thefederated read pathas a Steward capability.Other roles can keep using their normal graph/Nebula functions and never get a raw “search everything everywhere” bypass.

I think that’s the right boundary.

# Revised plan: do1 + 2 + 3 now, but4 lives inside Steward

## The governance rule

- Nebula MCPremains cabinet-facing: harvests, candidates, cross-refs, source material.
Nebula MCPremains cabinet-facing: harvests, candidates, cross-refs, source material.

- Knowledge MCPremains graph-facing: entities, edges, graph refs, curated semantic operations.
Knowledge MCPremains graph-facing: entities, edges, graph refs, curated semantic operations.

- Knowledge Stewardbecomes theonlyrole allowed to executecross-surface semantic federation.
Knowledge Stewardbecomes theonlyrole allowed to executecross-surface semantic federation.

So the system architecture becomes:

```
Other roles  ├─ can query Knowledge through graph functions  ├─ can query Nebula through cabinet functions  └─ cannot issue “search both and reconcile” directlyKnowledge Steward  ├─ can read Knowledge  ├─ can read Nebula  ├─ can use evidence bridge tables  ├─ can use semantic projection / embeddings  └─ owns unified semantic search + reconciliation suggestions
```

```
Other roles  ├─ can query Knowledge through graph functions  ├─ can query Nebula through cabinet functions  └─ cannot issue “search both and reconcile” directlyKnowledge Steward  ├─ can read Knowledge  ├─ can read Nebula  ├─ can use evidence bridge tables  ├─ can use semantic projection / embeddings  └─ owns unified semantic search + reconciliation suggestions
```

That gives you exactly the control boundary you want:the bridge exists in the substrate, but the bypass does not exist as a public capability.

---

# I’d now define the four linkages like this

# 1) Cross-schema cross-references

## Build now

But I’d slightly rename the concept in implementation to make the intent clearer:

- not“cross-schema refs” in the generic sense
not“cross-schema refs” in the generic sense

- yes“evidence links” / “knowledge evidence bridge”
yes“evidence links” / “knowledge evidence bridge”

### Purpose

Represent:

- a Nebula harvest candidate
a Nebula harvest candidate

- or harvest/source fragment
or harvest/source fragment

- asevidence abouta knowledge entity
asevidence abouta knowledge entity

### Ownership

This should be owned byknowledge/steward-side schema, not Nebula.

### Why

Because the statement:

> “harvest candidate X refines knowledge entity Y”

“harvest candidate X refines knowledge entity Y”

isnot a property of the harvest. It’s a semantic judgment made by the steward/knowledge layer.

---

# 2) Provenance bridge

## Build now

This is the second thing I’d do because it makes the evidence links auditable.

### Purpose

Support these traversals:

- knowledge entity -> supporting harvest candidates -> originating transcript
knowledge entity -> supporting harvest candidates -> originating transcript

- harvest candidate -> transcript source -> linked knowledge entities
harvest candidate -> transcript source -> linked knowledge entities

### Important distinction

I would keeptwo different kinds of relationshipseparate:

## A.Evidence link

“this candidate supports/refines/contradicts entity X”

## B.Provenance link

“this candidate came from transcript T / harvest H / span S”

That separation matters because later you’ll want to ask:

- “show me all evidence for this entity”
“show me all evidence for this entity”

- “show me where this candidate came from”
“show me where this candidate came from”

- “show me evidence that comes from this transcript”
“show me evidence that comes from this transcript”

Those are related, but not the same edge.

---

# 3) Embedding pipeline

## Build now

But I would not think of this as “add embeddings to Nebula.”I’d think of it as:

# Build a Steward-owned semantic projection layer over both systems

That gives you the bridge without giving every role direct access to it.

---

# The architecture I’d use

## A. Keep the source systems separate

- knowledge.*remains curated graph / semantic memory
knowledge.*remains curated graph / semantic memory

- nebula.*remains harvested cabinet / transcript-derived artifacts
nebula.*remains harvested cabinet / transcript-derived artifacts

## B. Add aSteward semantic workspace

Something like one of these:

- knowledge_steward.*
knowledge_steward.*

- steward.*
steward.*

- orknowledge.*if you want to keep it physically in the knowledge schema
orknowledge.*if you want to keep it physically in the knowledge schema

I’d lean toward a dedicated schema if you want the boundary to stay obvious:

# steward.*

Because the moment you say “only Steward gets the unified path,” it becomes useful to give Steward its own persistence layer for:

- bridge links
bridge links

- search projections
search projections

- reconciliation proposals
reconciliation proposals

- adjudication state
adjudication state

# Proposed schema split

## Keep existing

- knowledge.graph_entities
knowledge.graph_entities

- knowledge.graph_edges
knowledge.graph_edges

- knowledge.graph_cross_references
knowledge.graph_cross_references

- nebula.harvests_history
nebula.harvests_history

- nebula.cross_references
nebula.cross_references

- nebula.agent_records_history
nebula.agent_records_history

- etc.
etc.

## Add steward-owned bridge/projection tables

### steward.evidence_links

Maps Nebula evidence to Knowledge entities.

```
steward.evidence_links- id uuid pk- knowledge_entity_id uuid not null- nebula_harvest_id uuid null- nebula_candidate_id uuid null- link_type text not null    -- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | mentions- confidence numeric(5,4) null- rationale text null- status text not null default 'proposed'    -- proposed | accepted | rejected | superseded- created_by text not null- created_at timestamptz not null default now()- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'
```

```
steward.evidence_links- id uuid pk- knowledge_entity_id uuid not null- nebula_harvest_id uuid null- nebula_candidate_id uuid null- link_type text not null    -- supports | refines | instantiates | contradicts | mentions- confidence numeric(5,4) null- rationale text null- status text not null default 'proposed'    -- proposed | accepted | rejected | superseded- created_by text not null- created_at timestamptz not null default now()- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'
```

### steward.source_artifacts

Normalized provenance handles.

```
steward.source_artifacts- id uuid pk- source_system text not null    -- knowledge | nebula- source_kind text not null    -- kg_json_section | harvest | harvest_candidate | transcript | transcript_span- external_id text not null- filename text null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'
```

```
steward.source_artifacts- id uuid pk- source_system text not null    -- knowledge | nebula- source_kind text not null    -- kg_json_section | harvest | harvest_candidate | transcript | transcript_span- external_id text not null- filename text null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'
```

### steward.evidence_sources

Connect evidence links or entities to source artifacts.

```
steward.evidence_sources- id uuid pk- evidence_link_id uuid not null references steward.evidence_links(id)- source_artifact_id uuid not null references steward.source_artifacts(id)- relation_type text not null    -- extracted_from | cited_by | derived_from | spans
```

```
steward.evidence_sources- id uuid pk- evidence_link_id uuid not null references steward.evidence_links(id)- source_artifact_id uuid not null references steward.source_artifacts(id)- relation_type text not null    -- extracted_from | cited_by | derived_from | spans
```

### steward.semantic_documents

This is the semantic projection layer for embeddings/search.

```
steward.semantic_documents- id uuid pk- origin_system text not null    -- knowledge | nebula- origin_table text not null- origin_id text not null- document_kind text not null    -- knowledge_entity | harvest_candidate | transcript_chunk | agent_record- title text null- body text not null- embedding vector(1536) null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'- created_at timestamptz not null default now()- updated_at timestamptz not null default now()
```

```
steward.semantic_documents- id uuid pk- origin_system text not null    -- knowledge | nebula- origin_table text not null- origin_id text not null- document_kind text not null    -- knowledge_entity | harvest_candidate | transcript_chunk | agent_record- title text null- body text not null- embedding vector(1536) null- metadata jsonb not null default '{}'- created_at timestamptz not null default now()- updated_at timestamptz not null default now()
```

### Optional:steward.reconciliation_proposals

For “these things look like they should be linked / merged / updated.”

---

# Why I prefer asteward.*schema over stuffing this intoknowledge.*

Because your requirement is not just technical — it’sconstitutional.

You want:

- graph accessto remain graph access
graph accessto remain graph access

- cabinet accessto remain cabinet access
cabinet accessto remain cabinet access

- federated interpretationto be a privileged function of the steward
federated interpretationto be a privileged function of the steward

A dedicated schema makes that visible in both code and DB:

- knowledge= curated semantic memory
knowledge= curated semantic memory

- nebula= harvested records / cabinet
nebula= harvested records / cabinet

- steward= adjudication, evidence bridge, semantic federation
steward= adjudication, evidence bridge, semantic federation

That maps cleanly onto the role model you’re building.

---

- knowledge.semantic_search_union(...)as a public Knowledge MCP function
knowledge.semantic_search_union(...)as a public Knowledge MCP function

- nebula.semantic_search_union(...)
nebula.semantic_search_union(...)

a shared helper every role can call

### steward.semantic_recall(query, filters, mode)

or

### steward.find_related_knowledge(query, scope, evidence_policy)

where the steward internally does:

- searchsteward.semantic_documents
searchsteward.semantic_documents

- join back to:knowledge.graph_entitiesnebula.harvests_history/ candidate tables
join back to:

- knowledge.graph_entities
knowledge.graph_entities

- nebula.harvests_history/ candidate tables
nebula.harvests_history/ candidate tables

- pullsteward.evidence_links
pullsteward.evidence_links

- package the result asknowledge-grounded evidence, not raw search hits
package the result asknowledge-grounded evidence, not raw search hits

# The permission boundary I’d enforce

## Other roles can:

### Knowledge functions

- knowledge_list_entities
knowledge_list_entities

- knowledge_get_entity
knowledge_get_entity

- knowledge_get_neighbors
knowledge_get_neighbors

maybeknowledge_semantic_searchonly over curated KG entities, if you want that

list_harvests

- get_candidate
get_candidate

- list_cross_references
list_cross_references

- transcript / harvest browsing
transcript / harvest browsing

## Other roles cannot:

- querysteward.semantic_documents
querysteward.semantic_documents

- querysteward.evidence_linksdirectly unless you deliberately expose a narrow view
querysteward.evidence_linksdirectly unless you deliberately expose a narrow view

- issue cross-schema semantic search
issue cross-schema semantic search

- use “find me related stuff across everything” without going through Steward
use “find me related stuff across everything” without going through Steward

## Steward can:

- call both MCPs
call both MCPs

- readsteward.*
readsteward.*

- generate reconciliation proposals
generate reconciliation proposals

- run unified semantic retrieval
run unified semantic retrieval

- return graph-safe answers to other roles
return graph-safe answers to other roles

# The sequence I’d actually implement

# Phase A — Steward bridge substrate

## A1.steward.evidence_links

First artifact.

## A2.steward.source_artifacts+ provenance normalization

Second artifact.

## A3. backfill linker job

A batch job that proposes:

- harvest candidate → knowledge entity links
harvest candidate → knowledge entity links

- source transcript → knowledge entity provenance associations
source transcript → knowledge entity provenance associations

No mutation of KG yet; just create steward-side evidence records.

---

# Phase B — Steward semantic projection

## B1.steward.semantic_documents

Populate from:

### From Knowledge

- graph_entities.name
graph_entities.name

- description / notes / summary / source context
description / notes / summary / source context

- maybe edge-context summaries later
maybe edge-context summaries later

### From Nebula

- harvest candidateintent_description
harvest candidateintent_description

- classification summaries
classification summaries

- maybe transcript chunks later if worth it
maybe transcript chunks later if worth it

## B2. embedding generation pipeline

This can be a dedicated steward batch/worker:

- detect missing embeddings
detect missing embeddings

- generate embedding
generate embedding

- updatesteward.semantic_documents
updatesteward.semantic_documents

This is where I’d put the vector index, not on Nebula itself unless you have a separate Nebula-local use case.

---

# Phase C — Steward-only unified search

## C1. Build one internal API

Something like:

```
class StewardSemanticRecallService:    def search(        self,        query: str,        scopes: list[SemanticScope],        include_evidence: bool = True,        include_provenance: bool = True,        max_results: int = 20    ) -> StewardSearchResult:        ...
```

```
class StewardSemanticRecallService:    def search(        self,        query: str,        scopes: list[SemanticScope],        include_evidence: bool = True,        include_provenance: bool = True,        max_results: int = 20    ) -> StewardSearchResult:        ...
```

### Search scopes

- KNOWLEDGE_ENTITIES
KNOWLEDGE_ENTITIES

- HARVEST_CANDIDATES
HARVEST_CANDIDATES

- TRANSCRIPT_CHUNKS
TRANSCRIPT_CHUNKS

- AGENT_RECORDS
AGENT_RECORDS

### Result contract

Not raw documents. Return structured adjudication objects like:

```
StewardSearchHit(    hit_type="knowledge_entity" | "harvest_candidate" | "transcript_chunk",    origin_ref=...,    score=...,    related_knowledge_entities=[...],    supporting_evidence=[...],    provenance=[...])
```

```
StewardSearchHit(    hit_type="knowledge_entity" | "harvest_candidate" | "transcript_chunk",    origin_ref=...,    score=...,    related_knowledge_entities=[...],    supporting_evidence=[...],    provenance=[...])
```

That keeps the search result framed asSteward output, not “here’s the whole substrate, go nuts.”

---

# The key design rule I’d write down explicitly

## Unified semantic search is not a storage capability; it is a Steward reasoning capability.

That means:

- embeddings may live in steward-owned tables
embeddings may live in steward-owned tables

- bridge links may live in steward-owned tables
bridge links may live in steward-owned tables

- provenance may live in steward-owned tables
provenance may live in steward-owned tables

- search itself is exposed only through Steward-facing APIs
search itself is exposed only through Steward-facing APIs

This is exactly how you prevent the other roles from learning a “just bypass the graph and search everything” habit.

---

# So yes — I’d endorse your plan, with one refinement

## My refinement:

Don’t think of the first three as “bridges between knowledge and nebula” in the abstract.

Think of them as:

# Steward substrate

- evidence bridge
evidence bridge

- provenance bridge
provenance bridge

- semantic projection / embeddings
semantic projection / embeddings

- steward-only federated recall
steward-only federated recall

That keeps the boundary aligned with the social architecture of the system, not just the database architecture.

transformation.That’s better because it matches the rest of the system you’ve been describing:Steward is the sole semantic writerroles can request knowledgethe request goes through a policy/reconciliation boundaryNebula stays evidence/cabinet, not canonical truththe graph remains the committed state, but the recall path can consult more than the graphIf you want, I can now write the spec in the corrected form:Steward Recall Transformation Layer Spec v0with:shared publicKnowledgeRecallRequest/KnowledgeRecallResultinternalStewardRecallOrchestratorevidence_links,source_artifacts,semantic_documentsrole-based transformation pipelineaccepted/proposed evidence handlingMCP contract for “everyone can ask, Steward mediates”That’s probably the right artifact now, because the boundary has shifted fromwho may calltowhat gets injected into the call path.Sources

Thought for 6sYes — that’s a cleaner split, and it’s much closer to the rest of your architecture.What you’re describing now isnot “Steward is the only one who can do the query.”It’s:Everyone can invoke the same knowledge-recall operation.Steward owns thetransformation layer inside that operation.So the boundary moves fromAPI access controltoexecution semantics.I’d restate the model like thisPublic surface available to all rolesAll roles can make a knowledge request in the same general shape, e.g.knowledge_request(query, requester_role, scope, purpose, constraints)or even more simply:steward_recall(query, context)They all knowthatthis function exists and how to call it.What differs is what happensinsidethe callFor ordinary rolesThey call the same recall interface, but they donotknow or control:which schemas are searched firsthow Nebula and graph results are blendedwhat evidence-link heuristics are appliedwhat provenance filters or weighting rules are usedwhat transformation is inserted before results come backwhat role-weighting or policy shaping happened internallyThey knowthe contract, not the machinery.Steward’s special privilege is not “exclusive access to query”It’s:Steward can author / own / modify therecall transformation pipelineMeaning Steward knows about and controls:cross-schema evidence joinsprovenance expansionsemantic projection searchresult shaping / ranking / suppression / reconciliationgraph-safe interpretation of Nebula evidenceThat fits what you said earlier about Steward being thepolicy engine / kernel boundary / sole writerrather than just a locked door in front of search.So I’d update the architecture like thisOld framing I gave youother roles cannot bypass graph functionunified semantic search is Steward-onlyNew framing based on what you just saidall roles call the same knowledge-recall endpointthe endpoint is implemented by StewardSteward inserts the transformation/federation function into the call pathonly Steward understands or modifies the internal retrieval mechanicsThat’s a much better fit if DeepSeek is going to blaze ahead and you don’t want the rest of the system built around a fake “you’re not allowed to ask that question” boundary.The right mental model is thisSteward is aquery rewriter / federated resolver / policy transform, not just a privileged search client.So the pipeline becomes:Role-> knowledge request-> Steward recall boundary-> inject transformation / federation plan-> search graph + Nebula + evidence bridge + semantic projection-> apply role-weighting / provenance / policy shaping-> return result packageThat means the other roles know:how to askwhat kind of result to expectBut they donotknow:how the recall plan is assembledhow the evidence bridge is traversedhow embeddings are usedhow contradictions or duplicates are reconciledhow the final answer is normalized into graph-safe outputSo I would change the implementation plan accordinglyBuild 1–3 exactly as substrateBut don’t frame them as “Steward-private tables nobody else can conceptually know about.”Frame them asSteward-owned internals behind a shared recall contract.Revised implementation shapeShared public functionSomething like:PythonRunclassKnowledgeRecallService(Protocol):defrecall(self,query:str,requester_role:str,scope:RecallScope,purpose:str|None=None,constraints:dict|None=None) ->RecallResult:...Every role can call this.Steward internal pipeline behind that functionPythonRunclassStewardRecallOrchestrator:defrecall(self,request:KnowledgeRequest) ->RecallResult:plan=self._build_recall_plan(request)transformed=self._apply_role_transformations(request,plan)raw_hits=self._execute_federated_search(transformed)linked=self._attach_evidence_and_provenance(raw_hits,request)shaped=self._shape_for_requester(linked,request)returnshapedThe important part_apply_role_transformations(...)That’s the slot you just described.This is where Steward inserts the hidden function/transform into the request path.Examples of what belongs there:“if Topologist asks about graph structure, bias graph entities first and attach migration history second”“if Builder asks for implementation guidance, suppress low-confidence harvest chatter and prefer accepted knowledge entities plus supporting evidence”“if Observer asks an open question, include contradictions and unresolved candidates”“if a query smells like runtime context retrieval, don’t dump Nebula wholesale; return a curated evidence packet”So yes:same query surface, different internal transformation.Then the first three linkages become Steward internals supporting that transform1. Evidence bridgeUsed by the transform to answer:what harvest candidates support/refine this entity?what graph concepts are implicated by this Nebula material?2. Provenance bridgeUsed by the transform to answer:where did this claim come from?how much transcript backing exists?should this be surfaced as evidence or just as a weak lead?3. Semantic projection / embeddingsUsed by the transform to answer:what is semantically nearby across both curated and harvested material?what should be pulled into the candidate set before policy shaping?The boundary I would preserveRoles know:there is a recall functionit may search beyond the curated graphSteward mediates and shapes the answerreturned answers are not raw DB dumps but knowledge responsesRoles do not know / do not own:embedding store structurebridge-table semanticsranking heuristicsfederation plan constructionresult filtering / weighting internalsmutation/reconciliation rulesThat’s a very different kind of opacity than “you’re forbidden to ask.”So I’d revise my recommendation one more stepDon’t build a “Steward-only semantic search API.”Build asingle knowledge recall APIthat all roles use.But behind it, implement aSteward-owned recall enginewith these internal phases:Phase 1 — Candidate acquisitiongraph entity lookupsteward semantic document searchmaybe provenance/source expansionPhase 2 — Evidence expansionjoin toevidence_linksjoin to provenance/source artifactsattach supporting/contradicting harvest candidatesPhase 3 — Role transformrequester-role-specific weightingaccepted vs proposed evidence filteringgraph-first vs evidence-first shapingcontradiction handling rulesPhase 4 — Response shapingreturn a graph-safe knowledge packetnot raw blended recordsNet: I think this is the correct correctionYou’re not buildingsearch privilege.You’re buildingshared recall with Steward-owned internal query transformation.That’s better because it matches the rest of the system you’ve been describing:Steward is the sole semantic writerroles can request knowledgethe request goes through a policy/reconciliation boundaryNebula stays evidence/cabinet, not canonical truththe graph remains the committed state, but the recall path can consult more than the graphIf you want, I can now write the spec in the corrected form:Steward Recall Transformation Layer Spec v0with:shared publicKnowledgeRecallRequest/KnowledgeRecallResultinternalStewardRecallOrchestratorevidence_links,source_artifacts,semantic_documentsrole-based transformation pipelineaccepted/proposed evidence handlingMCP contract for “everyone can ask, Steward mediates”That’s probably the right artifact now, because the boundary has shifted fromwho may calltowhat gets injected into the call path.Sources

Yes — that’s a cleaner split, and it’s much closer to the rest of your architecture.

What you’re describing now isnot “Steward is the only one who can do the query.”It’s:

# Everyone can invoke the same knowledge-recall operation.

## Steward owns thetransformation layer inside that operation.

So the boundary moves fromAPI access controltoexecution semantics.

---

# I’d restate the model like this

## Public surface available to all roles

All roles can make a knowledge request in the same general shape, e.g.

```
knowledge_request(query, requester_role, scope, purpose, constraints)
```

```
knowledge_request(query, requester_role, scope, purpose, constraints)
```

or even more simply:

```
steward_recall(query, context)
```

```
steward_recall(query, context)
```

They all knowthatthis function exists and how to call it.

---

# What differs is what happensinsidethe call

## For ordinary roles

They call the same recall interface, but they donotknow or control:

- which schemas are searched first
which schemas are searched first

- how Nebula and graph results are blended
how Nebula and graph results are blended

- what evidence-link heuristics are applied
what evidence-link heuristics are applied

- what provenance filters or weighting rules are used
what provenance filters or weighting rules are used

- what transformation is inserted before results come back
what transformation is inserted before results come back

- what role-weighting or policy shaping happened internally
what role-weighting or policy shaping happened internally

They knowthe contract, not the machinery.

---

# Steward’s special privilege is not “exclusive access to query”

It’s:

## Steward can author / own / modify therecall transformation pipeline

Meaning Steward knows about and controls:

- cross-schema evidence joins
cross-schema evidence joins

- provenance expansion
provenance expansion

- semantic projection search
semantic projection search

- result shaping / ranking / suppression / reconciliation
result shaping / ranking / suppression / reconciliation

- graph-safe interpretation of Nebula evidence
graph-safe interpretation of Nebula evidence

That fits what you said earlier about Steward being thepolicy engine / kernel boundary / sole writerrather than just a locked door in front of search.

---

# So I’d update the architecture like this

## Old framing I gave you

- other roles cannot bypass graph function
other roles cannot bypass graph function

- unified semantic search is Steward-only
unified semantic search is Steward-only

## New framing based on what you just said

- all roles call the same knowledge-recall endpoint
all roles call the same knowledge-recall endpoint

- the endpoint is implemented by Steward
the endpoint is implemented by Steward

- Steward inserts the transformation/federation function into the call path
Steward inserts the transformation/federation function into the call path

- only Steward understands or modifies the internal retrieval mechanics
only Steward understands or modifies the internal retrieval mechanics

That’s a much better fit if DeepSeek is going to blaze ahead and you don’t want the rest of the system built around a fake “you’re not allowed to ask that question” boundary.

---

# The right mental model is this

# Steward is aquery rewriter / federated resolver / policy transform, not just a privileged search client.

So the pipeline becomes:

```
Role  -> knowledge request      -> Steward recall boundary          -> inject transformation / federation plan              -> search graph + Nebula + evidence bridge + semantic projection                  -> apply role-weighting / provenance / policy shaping                      -> return result package
```

```
Role  -> knowledge request      -> Steward recall boundary          -> inject transformation / federation plan              -> search graph + Nebula + evidence bridge + semantic projection                  -> apply role-weighting / provenance / policy shaping                      -> return result package
```

That means the other roles know:

- how to ask
how to ask

- what kind of result to expect
what kind of result to expect

But they donotknow:

- how the recall plan is assembled
how the recall plan is assembled

- how the evidence bridge is traversed
how the evidence bridge is traversed

- how embeddings are used
how embeddings are used

- how contradictions or duplicates are reconciled
how contradictions or duplicates are reconciled

- how the final answer is normalized into graph-safe output
how the final answer is normalized into graph-safe output

---

# So I would change the implementation plan accordingly

## Build 1–3 exactly as substrate

But don’t frame them as “Steward-private tables nobody else can conceptually know about.”Frame them asSteward-owned internals behind a shared recall contract.

---

# Revised implementation shape

## Shared public function

Something like:

```
class KnowledgeRecallService(Protocol):    def recall(        self,        query: str,        requester_role: str,        scope: RecallScope,        purpose: str | None = None,        constraints: dict | None = None    ) -> RecallResult:        ...
```

```
class KnowledgeRecallService(Protocol):    def recall(        self,        query: str,        requester_role: str,        scope: RecallScope,        purpose: str | None = None,        constraints: dict | None = None    ) -> RecallResult:        ...
```

Every role can call this.

---

# Steward internal pipeline behind that function

```
class StewardRecallOrchestrator:    def recall(self, request: KnowledgeRequest) -> RecallResult:        plan = self._build_recall_plan(request)        transformed = self._apply_role_transformations(request, plan)        raw_hits = self._execute_federated_search(transformed)        linked = self._attach_evidence_and_provenance(raw_hits, request)        shaped = self._shape_for_requester(linked, request)        return shaped
```

```
class StewardRecallOrchestrator:    def recall(self, request: KnowledgeRequest) -> RecallResult:        plan = self._build_recall_plan(request)        transformed = self._apply_role_transformations(request, plan)        raw_hits = self._execute_federated_search(transformed)        linked = self._attach_evidence_and_provenance(raw_hits, request)        shaped = self._shape_for_requester(linked, request)        return shaped
```

## The important part

### _apply_role_transformations(...)

That’s the slot you just described.

This is where Steward inserts the hidden function/transform into the request path.

Examples of what belongs there:

- “if Topologist asks about graph structure, bias graph entities first and attach migration history second”
“if Topologist asks about graph structure, bias graph entities first and attach migration history second”

- “if Builder asks for implementation guidance, suppress low-confidence harvest chatter and prefer accepted knowledge entities plus supporting evidence”
“if Builder asks for implementation guidance, suppress low-confidence harvest chatter and prefer accepted knowledge entities plus supporting evidence”

- “if Observer asks an open question, include contradictions and unresolved candidates”
“if Observer asks an open question, include contradictions and unresolved candidates”

- “if a query smells like runtime context retrieval, don’t dump Nebula wholesale; return a curated evidence packet”
“if a query smells like runtime context retrieval, don’t dump Nebula wholesale; return a curated evidence packet”

So yes:same query surface, different internal transformation.

---

# Then the first three linkages become Steward internals supporting that transform

## 1. Evidence bridge

Used by the transform to answer:

- what harvest candidates support/refine this entity?
what harvest candidates support/refine this entity?

- what graph concepts are implicated by this Nebula material?
what graph concepts are implicated by this Nebula material?

## 2. Provenance bridge

Used by the transform to answer:

- where did this claim come from?
where did this claim come from?

- how much transcript backing exists?
how much transcript backing exists?

- should this be surfaced as evidence or just as a weak lead?
should this be surfaced as evidence or just as a weak lead?

## 3. Semantic projection / embeddings

Used by the transform to answer:

- what is semantically nearby across both curated and harvested material?
what is semantically nearby across both curated and harvested material?

- what should be pulled into the candidate set before policy shaping?
what should be pulled into the candidate set before policy shaping?

---

# The boundary I would preserve

## Roles know:

- there is a recall function
there is a recall function

- it may search beyond the curated graph
it may search beyond the curated graph

- Steward mediates and shapes the answer
Steward mediates and shapes the answer

- returned answers are not raw DB dumps but knowledge responses
returned answers are not raw DB dumps but knowledge responses

## Roles do not know / do not own:

- embedding store structure
embedding store structure

- bridge-table semantics
bridge-table semantics

- ranking heuristics
ranking heuristics

- federation plan construction
federation plan construction

- result filtering / weighting internals
result filtering / weighting internals

- mutation/reconciliation rules
mutation/reconciliation rules

That’s a very different kind of opacity than “you’re forbidden to ask.”

---

# So I’d revise my recommendation one more step

## Don’t build a “Steward-only semantic search API.”

Build asingle knowledge recall APIthat all roles use.

But behind it, implement aSteward-owned recall enginewith these internal phases:

### Phase 1 — Candidate acquisition

- graph entity lookup
graph entity lookup

- steward semantic document search
steward semantic document search

- maybe provenance/source expansion
maybe provenance/source expansion

### Phase 2 — Evidence expansion

- join toevidence_links
join toevidence_links

- join to provenance/source artifacts
join to provenance/source artifacts

- attach supporting/contradicting harvest candidates
attach supporting/contradicting harvest candidates

### Phase 3 — Role transform

- requester-role-specific weighting
requester-role-specific weighting

- accepted vs proposed evidence filtering
accepted vs proposed evidence filtering

- graph-first vs evidence-first shaping
graph-first vs evidence-first shaping

- contradiction handling rules
contradiction handling rules

### Phase 4 — Response shaping

- return a graph-safe knowledge packet
return a graph-safe knowledge packet

- not raw blended records
not raw blended records

the request goes through a policy/reconciliation boundary
